## Draw Station (Trade Skills · `phlebotomy`)

**Category:** Trade Skills Simulator  
**Trade:** Phlebotomy technician  
**Union:** SEIU / NUHW — healthcare workers (hospital and laboratory locals)  
**Certification / standard claimed:** NHA CPT or ASCP PBT phlebotomy technician certification; CLSI GP41, the ANSI-approved American National Standard for collection of diagnostic venous blood specimens (two-identifier check, order of draw); ISO 6710 single-use evacuated blood collection tubes; OSHA Bloodborne Pathogens (29 CFR 1910.1030) and the Needlestick Safety and Prevention Act  
**Tagline:** Two-identifier check, venipuncture technique and the order of draw  
**Automated preview:** ✅ passed 11/11 checks on 2026-09-21 · **Practitioner sign-off:** ☐ pending

### Procedure as simulated

| # | Step | Kind | Rationale shown to the learner | Reviewer: correct? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Verify two patient identifiers | Select | Two identifiers, actively confirmed against the requisition in front of you — full name and date of birth, read off the band. Bed number is not one, and neither is a patient answering yes to a name called across the bay: someone drowsy, hard of hearing or confused will answer to anything, and GP41 puts the matching on you rather than on them. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 2 | Hand hygiene and gloves | Select | Gloves go on after hand hygiene, never instead of it — OSHA's bloodborne pathogens standard treats them as the last barrier, not the only one. They work in both directions: your hands away from this patient's blood, and the next patient away from whatever your hands picked up in the bay before this one. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 3 | Apply the tourniquet | Select | The tourniquet comes off inside a minute. Past that, trapped blood loses plasma water through the vessel wall and everything measured as a concentration climbs with it — potassium, calcium, total protein, packed cell volume. The result has already shifted before the needle goes in, and no one downstream can tell that from real disease. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 4 | Clean the site and let it dry | Select | Seventy per cent alcohol needs its contact time and then needs to air dry; the drying is part of the antisepsis and not a pause before it. Wet alcohol carried into the vein stings sharply and haemolyses the sample, and haemolysis releases potassium from the cells — enough to have a perfectly well patient treated for hyperkalaemia. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 5 | Insert bevel up at the right angle | Graded reading | Fifteen to thirty degrees, bevel up, with the vein anchored by your thumb below the site. Too shallow and the bevel skates over a vein that rolls away from it; too steep and you go through the back wall into tissue — or, working the medial side of the antecubital fossa, into the brachial artery and the median nerve lying alongside it. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 6 | Fill tubes in the order of draw | Sequence (strict order: blood culture, light blue — citrate, gold — SST, green — heparin, lavender — EDTA, grey — fluoride) | Additive carries over on the needle from one tube to the next, which is why the order is the control and why ISO 6710 colour-codes the closures at all. EDTA dragged into a chemistry tube chelates the calcium and sends potassium through the roof; a citrate tube drawn late or left short breaks the 9:1 blood-to-anticoagulant ratio every clotting result is calculated from. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 7 | Release the tourniquet | Select | The tourniquet comes off before the needle does. Pull out while the vein is still distended and the pressure behind it pushes blood into the surrounding tissue at the puncture site — that is the haematoma the patient shows everybody for a fortnight, and the reason they insist on the other arm next time. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 8 | Activate the safety device and dispose | Select | The safety device is engaged before your hand leaves the needle, and the needle goes straight into sharps — not onto the tray, not into a pocket, and never recapped. Almost every needlestick happens in the few seconds between the vein and the container, to a device nobody armed, which is exactly the gap engineered sharps protection was made law to close. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 9 | Hold pressure on the site | Timed hold | Direct pressure with the arm held straight until the bleeding has actually stopped — longer again if the patient is on warfarin or a direct oral anticoagulant. Bending the elbow over the gauze feels like pressure and is not: it holds the puncture open inside a fold of skin and fills the fossa with blood. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 10 | Label the tubes at the bedside | Select | Labelling happens here, at the chair, after the draw, with the patient still in front of you and the band still readable. Every metre between the tube and the person is a chance for it to become somebody else's sample, and a mislabelled tube arrives at the lab indistinguishable from a correct one — the error surfaces in the treatment it causes. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |

### Seeded hazards (unsafe actions)

| Hazard | Consequence shown to the learner | Reviewer: realistic? |
|---|---|---|
| `recap` | Never recap a used needle two-handed. That is the classic needlestick — activate the safety device and drop it straight into sharps. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `sharps-full` | That container is past the fill line. Forcing another sharp in is how the one already inside comes back out at your hand. Seal it and start a fresh one. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `iv-arm` | There is an infusion running in that arm. Drawing above an IV dilutes the sample with whatever is in the bag — wrong arm, wrong result. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `pre-labelled` | Those tubes were labelled before the draw. Pre-labelling is how samples get attributed to the wrong patient; tubes are labelled at the bedside, after they are filled. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |

## Weld Bay (Trade Skills · `welding`)

**Category:** Trade Skills Simulator  
**Trade:** Welder / fabricator  
**Union:** Boilermakers (IBB), Ironworkers and UA welders; AWS Certified Welder programme  
**Certification / standard claimed:** AWS D1.1 welder performance qualification; OSHA 29 CFR 1910.252 welding, cutting and brazing; NFPA 51B hot work permit and fire watch; ANSI Z49.1 fume control and lens shade  
**Tagline:** Hot work permit, fume extraction, lens shade, bead control and fire watch  
**Automated preview:** ✅ passed 11/11 checks on 2026-09-21 · **Practitioner sign-off:** ☐ pending

### Procedure as simulated

| # | Step | Kind | Rationale shown to the learner | Reviewer: correct? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Check the hot work permit | Select | The permit is the agreement that this area was surveyed and someone owns the watch. No permit, no arc. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 2 | Clear combustibles from the radius | Select | Combustibles go out to the required radius, or get covered. Sparks roll a long way across a shop floor. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 3 | Cover what cannot be moved | Select | Anything that cannot leave gets shielded. A blanket over the run is what stops a spark lodging in it unnoticed. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 4 | Position the fume extraction | Select | Extraction is positioned at the fume, not near it. Manganese and hexavalent chromium do their damage over a career, quietly. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 5 | Put on the full kit | Sequence (any order: welding helmet, leather jacket, welding gloves) | Arc burns skin like sun at close range and spatter goes everywhere. Partial PPE is the burn you explain later. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 6 | Set the lens shade | Graded reading | Too light and the arc reaches your eyes; too dark and you cannot see the puddle, so you chase it and ruin the bead. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 7 | Attach the work clamp | Select | The return path goes on the work, close to the weld. Letting the current find its own way through the bench and the building is how bearings and hinges get destroyed. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 8 | Set the amperage | Graded reading | Amperage follows electrode diameter and plate thickness. Too low and it sticks; too high and you burn through and undercut the toes. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 9 | Run the bead | Graded reading | Steady travel and a short arc length build an even bead. Racing gives you a thin ropey cap; crawling piles it up and traps slag. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 10 | Hold the fire watch | Timed hold | The watch continues after the arc stops, because that is when smouldering finds its way into something. Walking away at the last bead is how a shop burns down at 2 am. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |

### Seeded hazards (unsafe actions)

| Hazard | Consequence shown to the learner | Reviewer: realistic? |
|---|---|---|
| `solvent-drum` | There is an open solvent drum inside the hot work radius. Sparks travel further than people think and vapour ignites well below the flash you can see — it leaves the area or the work does. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `cylinder-loose` | That cylinder is lying unsecured with the cap off. A knocked-over cylinder with a sheared valve becomes a projectile — upright, chained, capped when not in use. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `wet-gloves` | Those gloves are damp. Wet leather conducts, and SMAW open-circuit voltage does not need much help to find a path through you. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `grinder-noguard` | The guard is off that grinder. A disc that lets go at full speed goes straight into whatever is in line with it, and that is usually your hand or your face. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `arc-bare` | You looked at the arc without the helmet down. Arc eye is a burn to the cornea and it does not announce itself for hours — the hood goes down before the arc is struck. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |

## Deploy Bay (Trade Skills · `devops`)

**Category:** Trade Skills Simulator  
**Trade:** Platform engineer / SRE  
**Union:** Non-union profession (CWA, through CODE-CWA and the Alphabet Workers Union, organises some technology workplaces)  
**Certification / standard claimed:** No licensing body governs a production deploy and nobody should pretend otherwise. What governs it is the site's own change-management procedure — the approved change window, the named approver, the recorded rollback — and the control frameworks that procedure is audited against: ISO/IEC 27001 for access control and change management, the SOC 2 change-management criteria, and NIST SP 800-53 AC-6 least privilege for automation identities. CWA organises some technology workplaces but certifies nobody for this. Vendor credentials (CNCF CKA, AWS DevOps Engineer) prove tool knowledge, not authority to ship.  
**Tagline:** Environment promotion, agent permission scoping, and a canary-gated rollout on a live deploy pipeline  
**Automated preview:** ✅ passed 11/11 checks on 2026-09-21 · **Practitioner sign-off:** ☐ pending

### Procedure as simulated

| # | Step | Kind | Rationale shown to the learner | Reviewer: correct? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Read the deploy ticket | Select | An agent runs exactly what it is told, at machine speed, and it will not stop halfway through to wonder whether this was a good idea. The ticket is where somebody wrote down what good looks like: the build, the scope, the approver and the rollback. If those four things are not on it, there is nothing here to supervise — only something to watch happen. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 2 | Clear the window before you start | Find (any order: the live incident board, the change calendar, the upstream deploy queue) | Most bad deploys are not bad code, they are good code shipped into a bad moment. An open incident, a change freeze or a dependency mid-rollout each make the canary unreadable, because you can no longer tell whether what you are seeing is your change or somebody else's. These take fifteen seconds to check now and hours to untangle afterwards. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 3 | Confirm the target environment | Select | Staging and production run the same tooling, the same dashboards and very nearly the same hostnames, and at two in the morning they look identical. The label is the only thing standing between a test and an outage, which is why it gets read deliberately rather than assumed from which tab was open when you sat down. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 4 | Verify environment-scoped secrets | Sequence (any order: database credential, API key, webhook secret) | A secret scoped to the wrong environment is not an error anybody sees: the job runs, the tests pass, and a staging pipeline has just read or written real customer data with a production credential. All three get checked because it is always the third one — the webhook nobody thinks of as a secret — that is still pointing at prod. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 5 | Issue a least-privilege token | Carry and place | Least privilege is not paperwork, it is the size of the hole in the wall when something goes wrong. A token scoped to this service, this action and this window means the worst a confused agent can do is break this deploy; a standing admin key means the worst it can do is anything the account can do, for as long as the key lives. The admin key beside it will work perfectly, which is the problem with it. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 6 | Review the agent's proposed change | Select | Approving automation output you have not read is how a bad change ships with a human's name on the approval and nobody able to say what it did. Read the diff for what it removes as much as what it adds — a dropped feature flag or a halved timeout is one line, does not look like anything, and is the change that takes the service down at peak. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 7 | Open the canary to 10% | Turn | A canary works because real traffic is the only load test that is actually representative, and ten per cent of it is small enough that the failure you are looking for is survivable. It also has to be big enough to see: a canary at a fraction of a per cent produces error rates that are statistically indistinguishable from nothing, which reads as success. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 8 | Watch the canary error rate | Graded reading | This is the only checkpoint in the whole pipeline where the new build has met real users and the blast radius is still one in ten. A canary that is already unhealthy here is a full outage at a hundred per cent, and the number does not settle instantly — give it long enough to be a reading rather than the first spike of a cold cache, then commit on what it actually says. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 9 | Promote to full traffic | Turn | Promotion is a separate, deliberate act from opening the canary, on a separate control, because the two decisions are different: one says let us find out, the other says I have looked and I am satisfied. Wire them into a single motion and the canary becomes a delay rather than a gate — which is exactly what it turns into on the deploys nobody remembers going wrong. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 10 | Arm and test the rollback | Timed hold | A rollback nobody has exercised is a hope with a button on it. Test it now, while the service is healthy and you have all the time in the world, because the alternative is finding out that the previous build's image was garbage-collected at the one moment you needed it — at which point the only way out is forward, through a fix written by somebody who has been awake for nineteen hours. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 11 | Log the change and the agent's actions | Select | Months from now somebody will be reading this line to answer a question nobody has asked yet: which build introduced the regression, what did the automation have access to, who decided it was fine. Written at the end it records what actually happened, including the parts that did not go to plan — which is the only version worth keeping. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |

### Seeded hazards (unsafe actions)

| Hazard | Consequence shown to the learner | Reviewer: realistic? |
|---|---|---|
| `prod-console` | That tab is the production console, not staging — it renders identically at a glance, which is exactly how a 2 a.m. mistake happens. Close it and confirm you're back in staging before touching anything. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `prod-db-secret` | That credential is scoped to production, not staging. Handing an automated job a secret for the wrong environment lets a test run read or write real customer data. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `admin-token` | That's a standing admin credential, not a scoped token for this job. An automation only needs the access this run requires — a broad, long-lived key turns any bug in its logic into an account-wide blast radius. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `break-glass` | That is the break-glass override, which ships a change without an approver and without a window. It exists for an incident already in progress, it pages the whole on-call chain when it is used, and every audit of this pipeline starts by asking who pulled it and why. Using it to save four minutes on a routine promote is a control failure whether or not the deploy works. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `mute-alerts` | Muting alerting for the deploy window turns the canary into a decoration. The entire reason you route a sliver of traffic at a new build is so the paging system tells you it is broken before the rest of the traffic finds out — silence it and you have kept the ceremony and thrown away the signal. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |

What governs it is the site's own change-management procedure — the approved change window, the named approver, the recorded rollback — and the control frameworks that procedure is audited against: ISO/IEC 27001 for access control and change management, the SOC 2 change-management criteria, and NIST SP 800-53 AC-6 least privilege for automation identities. CWA organises some technology workplaces but certifies nobody for this. Vendor credentials (CNCF CKA, AWS DevOps Engineer) prove tool knowledge, not authority to ship. |

## Rough-In Bay (Trade Skills · `plumbing`)

**Category:** Trade Skills Simulator  
**Trade:** Plumber / Pipefitter  
**Union:** UA — United Association of Plumbers, Pipefitters and Service Technicians  
**Certification / standard claimed:** UA journeyman plumber and state plumbing licence; IPC/UPC drain-waste-vent code; ANSI/ASSE 5110 backflow prevention assembly tester and ANSI/ASSE 1020 pressure vacuum breakers; NSF/ANSI 61 and 372 for wetted drinking-water components; ASME B31.9 building services piping; OSHA 29 CFR 1910.252 welding, cutting and brazing with NFPA 51B hot work  
**Tagline:** DWV rough-in, backflow prevention, torch brazing and a pressure test with no shortcuts  
**Automated preview:** ✅ passed 11/11 checks on 2026-09-21 · **Practitioner sign-off:** ☐ pending

### Procedure as simulated

| # | Step | Kind | Rationale shown to the learner | Reviewer: correct? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Read the rough-in work order | Select | Fixture units, vent size and where the wet vent picks up all get confirmed before a saw touches the framing. An undersized vent shows up as a trap siphoning itself dry six months later, venting sewer gas into a finished bathroom through a wall that is by then boarded and tiled. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 2 | Close the main shutoff | Turn | Assume street pressure on every fixture downstream. The branch valve you believe isolates this run may be a fifteen-year-old gate that no longer seats, and you would only learn that at the first cut. Take the main round to its stop instead of trusting a valve nobody has ever proved. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 3 | Bleed the line | Timed hold | A closed main does not empty a riser. The column hangs on its own vacuum until something admits air, so the first fitting cracked becomes the vent and the whole standing head lands at once — on the floor, the tools and anything open below. Bleed it down and watch the flow die. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 4 | Dry-fit the DWV run | Sequence (strict order: vent stack, closet bend, P-trap) | Drain, waste and vent assembles top down: the vent tie-in and the fall are the datum everything under them is set from. The trap goes last because its weir height and arm slope decide whether the seal holds or siphons out, and neither can be adjusted once the stack and bend are welded in. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 5 | Fit the backflow preventer | Carry and place | A hose bib is the classic cross-connection — the one outlet a garden hose, a bucket and a momentary pressure drop can turn into a feed running backwards into potable supply. A vacuum breaker severs that path mechanically, which is why code names this fitting specifically rather than trusting the pressure. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 6 | Solvent-weld the PVC joint | Timed hold | Solvent cement does not glue PVC — it softens both faces so they fuse as the solvent flashes off. Hold the joint bottomed out while that happens, because the pipe's own spring-back backs it a few millimetres out of the socket and leaves a void the cement never closes. That is the joint that tests fine and weeps in service. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 7 | Dial a neutral flame | Graded reading | Neutral means exactly enough oxygen to consume the fuel, with nothing spare at the tip. Carburising soots the fitting so flux cannot wet it; oxidising strips the flux ahead of the solder and grows an oxide film that solder simply refuses to bond to. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 8 | Sweat the copper joint | Graded reading | Capillary action drags molten solder right round a properly heated fitting unaided — heat the copper, not the solder, and the joint draws when it is ready. Chase it with the flame and you get a tidy fillet at the mouth, voids behind it, and a weep that surfaces either at test or inside a finished wall. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 9 | Hold the test pressure | Graded reading | Code names a test pressure and a duration, and the duration is the half that catches anything — everything looks tight for ten seconds. A fitting shedding a pound over fifteen minutes will drip for years behind drywall, and the callback to chase it costs more than this rough-in earned. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| 10 | Find the weeping joint | Find (any order: weeping joint) | A falling needle says the system is shedding air and says nothing whatever about where. Locating it stays a fitting-by-fitting visual, and this is the last opportunity anyone has: after the board goes up, the same defect is diagnosed from a stain on a ceiling below, through somebody else's finishes. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |

### Seeded hazards (unsafe actions)

| Hazard | Consequence shown to the learner | Reviewer: realistic? |
|---|---|---|
| `hose-bucket` | That garden hose runs from the hose bib straight into the mop bucket, submerged. Let street pressure drop for a second and that bucket water siphons back into the supply — an air gap or a backflow preventer is what stops it, not hoping the pressure never drops. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `torch-stud` | That torch tip is inches from a bare wood stud with no heat shield behind the joint. Sweating a fitting against dry framing is exactly how a slow smoulder starts inside a wall — one nobody notices until hours after you've packed up. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `pressurized-union` | That union is still showing line pressure on the gauge. Crack a fitting under pressure and whatever's behind it — water, or a still-hot line — comes out at whoever's hands are on the wrench. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
| `solvent-torch` | The primer and cement are open right next to a lit torch. Both are flammable solvents sitting open — vapour finding that flame is not a maybe, it's a when. | ☐ yes ☐ no — |
